Top Songs of 2021

The top songs of 2021 reflected a diverse and dynamic year in music, blending emotional storytelling with genre experimentation and viral momentum. From pop powerhouses and hip-hop icons to global superstars and emerging talent, 2021 was defined by artists who dominated both charts and social media feeds. Streaming services and TikTok continued to shape musical trends, helping songs become global sensations within days.

With massive hits from Olivia Rodrigo, The Weeknd, Doja Cat, and Lil Nas X, alongside international success from BTS and Bad Bunny, the 2021 songs list captured a year of reinvention, connection, and bold expression. Whether you’re rediscovering 2021 hit songs or diving in for the first time, this archive features the complete weekly Top 40 rankings from one of the most culturally significant years in modern music.

How We Ranked the Top Songs of 2021

*Note: Based on the Nolan Method, these are the year-end best songs of 2021 that represented the Top 40 songs during the calendar year 2021. The Nolan Method ranking system was formulated by lead Top40Weekly contributor Jarrett Nolan and is based on a point system for an artist’s chart performance on Billboard charts.

Notable Songs & Artists from 2021

With genre-blending collaborations, emotionally resonant storytelling, and a music landscape driven by digital engagement, the top songs of 2021 marked a year where artists took risks, broke records, and connected with audiences on a deeper, global scale.

Olivia Rodrigo’s Breakout Year

Few artists made as big an impact in 2021 as Olivia Rodrigo. Her debut single “drivers license” debuted at No. 1 on the Billboard Hot 100 and broke multiple streaming records, becoming a global anthem for heartbreak. She followed it up with “good 4 u”, a fiery pop-punk anthem that showcased her versatility and further cemented her as the voice of a new pop generation. Her debut album SOUR became one of the biggest releases of the year, influencing a fresh wave of emotional, confessional songwriting in mainstream pop.

Hip-Hop and R&B Continue to Thrive

Hip-hop and R&B maintained dominance on the charts in 2021, with artists blending genre boundaries and experimenting with new sounds. Drake’s “Way 2 Sexy”, featuring Future and Young Thug, became an instant viral hit, while The Weeknd’s retro-tinged “Save Your Tears”—especially the remix featuring Ariana Grande—sustained his reign at the top of the pop-R&B crossover space. Doja Cat, one of the year’s biggest stars, delivered “Kiss Me More” (featuring SZA), a smooth blend of R&B, disco, and pop that topped charts and dominated playlists.

Lil Nas X & the Power of Virality

Lil Nas X once again proved his ability to blend controversy, creativity, and hit-making. His single “MONTERO (Call Me By Your Name)” sparked cultural conversations and delivered a bold visual aesthetic, all while climbing to No. 1. He followed it up with “Industry Baby”, featuring Jack Harlow, a triumphant, brass-filled anthem that quickly became one of the most streamed and celebrated tracks of the year.

Global Hits: BTS, Latin Music & More

Global influence continued to shape the biggest songs of 2021. BTS dominated with their English-language singles “Butter” and “Permission to Dance”, each topping charts and reinforcing their global pop dominance. Meanwhile, Bad Bunny continued to lead the Latin music movement with tracks like “Yonaguni”, while Camila Cabello brought tropical flair with “Don’t Go Yet”, showcasing the mainstream staying power of reggaeton and Latin pop.
